{"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "items_dropped=list(range(I)),\n        )\n\n    res = girth.grm_mml(X)\n    ability = np.asarray(res[\"Ability\"], dtype=float)\n    discrimination = np.asarray(res[\"Discrimination\"], dtype=float)\n    difficulty = np.asarray(res[\"Difficulty\"], dtype=float)\n\n    items_dropped = [int(i) for i, a in enumerate(discrimination) if not np.isfinite(a) or a <= 0]\n    # Replace non-finite discriminations with a small positive value\n    discrimination = np.where(np.isfinite(discrimination) & (discrimination > 0), discrimination, 1e-3)\n\n    return GRMResult(\n        ability=ability,\n        discrimination=discrimination,\n        difficulty=difficulty,\n        convergence_iters=int(getattr(res, \"iterations\", 0) or 0),\n        aic=float(res.get(\"AIC\", {}).get(\"final\", float(\"nan\")) if isinstance(res.get(\"AIC\"), dict) else float(res.get(\"AIC\", float(\"nan\")))),\n        bic=float(res.get(\"BIC\", {}).get(\"final\", float(\"nan\")) if isinstance(res.get(\"BIC\"), dict) else float(res.get(\"BIC\", float(\"nan\")))),\n        n_categories=K,\n        items_dropped=items_dropped,\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Stage 5: Validation A \u2014 correlation with published accuracy\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef validate_a(grm: GRMResult, panel: list[str]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Compute Pearson r between theta_j and published MMLU accuracy.\n\n    Includes bootstrap CI and per-model breakdown.\n    \"\"\"\n    mmlu = np.array([PUBLISHED_ACCURACY[m][\"mmlu\"] for m in panel], dtype=float)\n    helm = np.array([PUBLISHED_ACCURACY[m][\"helm\"] for m in panel], dtype=float)\n    theta = np.asarray(grm.ability, dtype=float)\n\n    def pearson(x, y):\n        if len(x) < 3 or np.std(x) == 0 or np.std(y) == 0:\n            return float(\"nan\")\n        return float(np.corrcoef(x, y)[0, 1])\n\n    r_mmlu = pearson(theta, mmlu)\n    r_helm = pearson(theta, helm)\n\n    # Fisher z-transform CI\n    def ci(x, y, n_boot=10000, seed=0):\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n        rs = []\n        n = len(x)\n        for _ in range(n_boot):\n            idx = rng.integers(0, n, size=n)\n            xx, yy = x[idx], y[idx]\n            if np.std(xx) == 0 or np.std(yy) == 0:\n                continue\n            rs.append(pearson(xx, yy))\n        if not rs:\n            return (float(\"nan\"), float(\"nan\"))\n        lo, hi = np.percentile(rs, [2.5, 97.5])\n        return (float(lo), float(hi))\n\n    ci_mmlu = ci(theta, mmlu)\n    ci_helm = ci(theta, helm)\n\n    return {\n        \"r_mmlu\": r_mmlu,\n        \"r_helm\": r_helm,\n        \"ci_mmlu\": ci_mmlu,\n        \"ci_helm\": ci_helm,\n        \"theta_ranking\": sorted(\n            [{\"model\": m, \"theta\": float(t), \"mmlu\": float(mmlu[i]), \"helm\": float(helm[i])}\n             for i, (m, t) in enumerate(zip(panel, theta))],\n            key=lambda d: -d[\"theta\"],\n        ),\n        \"success_r_gt_0_85\": bool(r_mmlu > 0.85),\n        \"n_models\": len(panel),\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Stage 6: Validation B \u2014 contamination simulation"}], "is_error": false, "command": "sed -n '280,360p' /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_56IKXpJEZJ5b/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_1/scoring_grm.py", "exit_code": 0, "timeout": false, "metadata": {"exit_code": 0, "pid": -1, "username": "root", "hostname": "c7dc3f2fb43a", "working_dir": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_56IKXpJEZJ5b/3_invention_loop/iter_2/review_paper/review_paper", "py_interpreter_path": "/usr/local/bin/python", "prefix": "", "suffix": "\n[The command completed with exit code 0.]"}, "full_output_save_dir": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_56IKXpJEZJ5b/.oh_sessions/6730b295d8f54946b402ab08670da7a7/observations", "kind": "TerminalObservation"}

Total pairs = 10, discordant = 1 (Mistral-Qwen swap in the middle).\n- Spearman \u03c1 = 1 - 6*(sum of d^2)/n/(n^2-1) = 1 - 6*1/5/24 = 1 - 6/120 = 1 - 0.05 = 0.95\n\nHmm but the paper says \u03c1 = 0.90. Let me check again.\n\nActually the formula for Spearman \u03c1 when there are ties is more complex. With n = 5 and one rank swap, the calculation depends on the actual rank positions:\n- GPT-4o-mini: rank 1 (both metrics)\n- Claude Haiku 4.5: rank 2 (both metrics)\n- Mistral Small 24B: rank 3 by MMLU, rank 4 by \u03b8 (inversion)\n- Qwen 2.5 7B: rank 4 by MMLU, rank 3 by \u03b8 (inversion)\n- Llama 3.1 8B: rank 5 (both metrics)\n\nSo the rank differences are 0, 0, -1, +1, 0. Sum of d^2 = 0+0+1+1+0 = 2.\nSpearman \u03c1 = 1 - 6*2/(5*(25-1)) = 1 - 12/120 = 1 - 0.1 = 0.90. \u2713\n\nFor Kendall \u03c4: with n = 5, there are C(5,2) = 10 pairs. The single swap creates:\n- 1 discordant pair (Mistral vs Qwen: their order is different in the two rankings)\n- 9 concordant pairs\n\n\u03c4 = (concordant - discordant) / total = (9 - 1) / 10 = 0.80. \u2713\n\nOK so the rank statistics are correctly derived (0.90 and 0.80) and they bound the rank claim at one swap.\n\nNow the seed-only accuracy and paraphrase agreement would have similar rank structure:\n- Seed-only acc: GPT-4o-mini (0.89), Claude Haiku 4.5 (0.90), Mistral Small 24B (0.83), Qwen 2.5 7B (0.78), Llama 3.1 8B (0.65)\n- MMLU ranking: GPT-4o-mini > Claude > Mistral > Qwen > Llama\n- Seed-only ranking: Claude > GPT-4o-mini > Mistral > Qwen > Llama\n\nSo for seed-only vs MMLU:\n- GPT-4o-mini: rank 1 (MMLU), rank 2 (seed-only) \u2014 discordant\n- Claude Haiku 4.5: rank 2 (MMLU), rank 1 (seed-only) \u2014 discordant\n- Mistral Small 24B: rank 3 (both) \u2014 concordant\n- Qwen 2.5 7B: rank 4 (both) \u2014 concordant\n- Llama 3.1 8B: rank 5 (both) \u2014 concordant\n\nSo one swap (GPT-Claude swap at the top). \u03c1 = 0.90, \u03c4 = 0.80. \u2713\n\nOK so the rank statistics match. Yet current LLMs often show inconsistencies in their judgments. In this work, we examine logical preference consistency as a foundational requirement for building more dependable LLM systems, ensuring stable and coherent decision-making while minimizing erratic or contradictory outputs. To quantify the logical preference consistency, we propose a universal evaluation framework based on three fundamental properties: transitivity, commutativity and negation invariance. Through extensive experimentation across diverse LLMs, we demonstrate that these properties serve as strong indicators of judgment robustness. Furthermore, we introduce a data refinement and augmentation technique, REPAIR, that enhances logical consistency while maintaining alignment with human preferences. Finally, we show that improving consistency leads to better performance in LLM-driven logic-based algorithms, reinforcing stability and coherence in decision-making systems.\" />\n {
  "message": "I've submitted my adversarial review of the M-IRT paper at `/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_56IKXpJEZJ5b/3_invention_loop/iter_2/review_paper/review_paper/.sdk_openhands_agent_struct_out.json`.\n\n**Summary of assessment:**\n\n**Overall score: 4 (Borderline Reject) | Confidence: 4**\n\n**Key improvements over iter-1 that I acknowledged:**\n- The paper now properly cites the consistency literature (Liu et al. 2024, BECEL/Jang et al., Novikova et al., ConsistencyGate, Musawi & Lu) and distinguishes M-IRT via polytomous/dynamic/contamination-resistance.\n- Table 3 with binary consistency baselines (paraphrase agreement, negation invariance, composite) is reproducible from the artifact to the third decimal place \u2014 I verified by computing all values from `exp_eval_sol_out.json` and `method_out.json`.\n- The numerical discrepancies between paper text and artifact (mean \u0394\u03b8, std, static acc) are fixed.\n\n**Three blocking issues that remain (score-impact ordered):**\n\n1. **[MAJOR \u2014 methodology]** The arithmetic negation variant is NOT reference-free. In `mic_generator.py:366`, the negation oracle uses `wrong = seed_answer + 1`, meaning determining the oracle (NO) requires computing the seed answer. The paper claims it's \"derivable from the question text alone\" \u2014 this is false for half the clusters.\n\n2. **[MAJOR \u2014 evidence]** The headline Pearson r = 0.975 is statistically vacuous on n = 5 (bootstrap CI [0.59, 1.0]); Spearman \u03c1 = 0.90 is bounded by exactly one rank inversion (Mistral-Qwen swap). The paper should expand to n \u2265 15 ($0.20-0.50 cost) and lead with rank statistics.\n\n3. **[MAJOR \u2014 methodology]** The contamination-resistance experiment modifies the response matrix in memory but does NOT re-query the model \u2014 it tests an artificial pattern (\"correct seed + wrong paraphrase\") that no realistic memorization produces. The natural-contamination protocol should be implemented as the main result.\n\n4. **[MAJOR \u2014 scope]** Only two MIC domains (syllogisms + arithmetic) cannot probe MMLU's history/law/medicine/biology/philosophy domains. Need multi-hop reading + first-order logic + ideally analogy.\n\n**Minor issues also flagged:** numerical errors in Section 5.5 (62/14/24 should be 67/24/9), K=3 ordinal mapping not justified empirically, abstract overclaims about contamination-resistance, forward-dated citations to verify, and an unexplained Mistral-Qwen rank inversion.\n\nThe work is conceptually promising \u2014 the dynamic-generation + relational-invariants + GRM framing is a genuine re-thinking of the evaluation problem. But the methodology bug in arithmetic negation, the statistical insufficiency at n = 5, and the artificial contamination test mean the contribution is currently a proof-of-concept rather than a foundational result."
}
